Before I begin my words, I salute the High Council. Dear lawmakers, our country is unfortunately a country where mining accidents occur a lot. The biggest disaster ever caused by the mines was the Soma disaster that we lost 263 miners on March 3, 1992, and 301 mine workers who drowned our entire country on May 17, 2014. As in all our provinces, where coal mines have been mined, there is only one request for everyone in our Zonguldak province: that they rise early in the morning from their warm bed and return safely to their homes in the evening, as if by hundreds of feet below the ground, with their nails to earn their sustenance. In the process that has come to this day, our loyal and painful mining brothers and sisters have planned their mining policies to reduce our economic dependence on the world by enforcing our economic growth by considering international standards, business health and safety. Furthermore, giving private sector miners the incentives they've been waiting for for for many years has also been given to the power of the EC PARTY. In our country, only the Zonguldak and the surrounding stone coal have been encouraged by our government. In this way, the private sector has contributed to the extraction of our precious stone coal in our province. In our time, the bag of legislation adopted on 10 September 2014 improved the working conditions of our miners. The hours are 36 hours a week, and the fees are not less than 2 minimum wage. Those who work in the underworld for one day are entitled to severance pay. Retirement age has been reduced from 55 to 50. Finally, the Mining Law, adopted on February 4, 2015, also made some legal arrangements for the miners. The price hikes from the rights given to miners by the bag law have been secured by the Council of Ministers. We know that there is work to address some of the difficulties in this area, which have been partly expressed in the pre-accession of the EC PARTI authorities, with some laws being passed. In our time, we see in the commission report on the Soma disaster, where we can't forget how effective the human element is, even though there are laws at the point of a permanent solution to problems in this area. Turkey's Taşkomömu Agency has requested 1,400 workers in 2011 to increase the production of stone coal, which is one of the main sources of our province's livelihood, which has given the country's economy serious momentum. However, because this demand was not met and due to the continuation of pensions, the number of workers he requested in 2012 increased to 2.492. Due to the continued pensions at the Turkish Stone Coal Foundation and the lack of new recruitment, production has decreased, labour deficits have increased, which has become a threat to occupational health and safety. For this reason, our efforts as city lawmakers continue to address labour deficits at the Turkish Taşkomuru Agency. In our Zonguldak province, every house has a wife or son who works in a mine. The Zonguldak woman who sends her wife and son to the mine waits with hope for a safe return. Once again, I would like to express from this bench that the dedication of mine workers who work hundreds of feet below the ground in the world's most heavy and risky line of work is above all recognition. Dear lawmakers, I ask that all miners who sweat with their lives in the mines should be made easy by the Almighty God, as we ask God to grant us the mercy of over 5,000 of our mining martyrs who have so far lost their lives in the mining sector. With such feelings and thoughts, I wish that such disasters would never happen again in our country and in the world, and I salute the High Council.

At the Sarp Border Gate, there have been major queues, especially as a result of the increase in passport-free crossings, and many victims of the waste of time by commercial businessmen have suffered financial and spiritual losses. At the same time, the Sarp Border Gate, which is the international gate, where many tourists enter and exit our country, is also important for the prestige of our country. Over 24 hours of waiting, and our border door, where there are great riots, also gives a negative impression of our country's image. The start of passport-free crossings in your government at the time of your government ($ I.D.) and the desire of our citizens to travel to Georgia for the benefit of these transitions are causing massive piles. The cause of these collapses is the transition points, which have been newly built, but are unplanned, unprojected. In the neighbouring country, there are nearly 20 processing points, while our country's number of platforms is only 2. There is a big difference between the time a tourist lives at the gates of Turkey and the waste of his time living in a Georgia port. I would like to point out in particular that the reason for this is the use of methods that are not in line with the conditions of the day. In addition, the last facility at the Sarp Border Gate was opened by Prime Minister Recep Tayyip Erdogan after four years of renovations, but still problems at the border crossing have not been resolved. These challenges are being experienced because the border gate is not designed according to geographical structure and land. There are two truck parks, but sometimes there are car lines all the way to the port village of Kemalpaşa. Tails are causing traffic density and disruptions, and many times even sad accidents occur. In view of the above, the Sarp Border Gate is inadequate to our country, to sum up the negatives. There are many reasons for waiting for more than 24 hours of car piles, not responding to the conditions of the day, not being able to count the number of people working on platforms, and many reasons for not counting the lack of staff, the irregularity of vehicle entry and exits, living at the Sarp Border Gate. Now, the European Court of Human Rights rulings say that political parties are an indispensable element of democracy, political parties are necessary for a pluralistic democracy. One country's problems are solved only by dialogue between political parties, all sorts of ideas arise only through this dialogue, and this dialogue needs to be done freely. Political parties can only be considered exceptional if they pose a threat to democracy and have concrete evidence of it. The European Court of Human Rights ▪ Political parties may recommend changing the constitutional structure of a law or a state. This is entirely possible, but there are two conditions: one is that the goal is to be in line with democracy, the principles of democracy; and the second is that the methods to achieve that goal are democratic. Under this condition, even the constitutional structure of a state can be proposed by political parties. Now, when you came to close the DEP, the closure of the DEP will also be returned from the European Court of Human Rights. The European Court of Human Rights ruled it a violation of Article 11 of political parties. The Constitution's provisions on the closure of political parties are allowed to be closed as a result of the political party's actions or as a result of the programme. Article 68 of the Constitution states that it is possible to close political parties due to regulations and programmes. It is an issue not accepted by the European Court of Human Rights. Political parties cannot be shut down due to their programmes or regulations. This is a very clear issue in the case of the United Communist Party and the Socialist Party. Therefore, it is necessary to change Article 68 of the Constitution, in accordance with the European Court of Human Rights decision. On 2 July 1992, Law 3835 and the Turks of Ahiska were settled in Turkey. According to Article 6 of this Law, it has been corrected by law, and it has been corrected by the new law they remain in both Turkey and abroad, and it has been decided that those who will be identified by the Council of Ministers of Ahıska who will remain in the states would be recognised by Turkish citizenship. In accordance with the amendment made in Law 3835, Turkey's Ahıska Turks, who resided in Turkey before January 1, 2009, were granted Turkish citizenship status within six months of the application date, if you do not mind national security, if you do not mind them applying within three months of the implementation of this clause. As part of this law, many Ahiska Turks were granted Turkish citizenship, but thousands of Ahiska Turks were not able to benefit from it and were unable to obtain the documents they were asked for during the deadline and were unable to become Turkish citizens. A new regulation of the relevant law must be made in order to prevent the victimization of the Turkish non-Turkish Ahiska. Here, with this proposal of law, we propose that Turkish citizenship be granted to the Turkish Turks who reside in Turkey on January 1st or after 2009, provided they provide certain conditions.
